\delta:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, and therefore
the composition
e^{\delta}
is defined in
\{x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mid\exists z\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}:\ \delta(x)\leq\log z\}
,
which of course does not contain
x=0
but only suitable non zero
infinitesimals. On the other hand,
\delta\circ\delta:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.
Moreover, from the inclusion of ordinary smooth functions (Thm.
18
)
and the closure with respect to composition, it directly follows that
every
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(U,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
is an algebra with pointwise operations
for every subset
U\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

A natural way to define a GSF is to follow the original idea of classical
authors (see
[
47
,
19
]
) to fix an infinitesimal or infinite
parameter in a suitable ordinary smooth function. We will call this
type of GSF of
Cauchy-Dirac type
; the next theorem specifies
this notion and states that GSF are of Cauchy-Dirac type whenever
the generating net
(f_{\varepsilon})
is smooth in
\varepsilon
.

Corollary 21
.

Let
X\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
Y\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

P\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
be open sets and
\varphi\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(P\times X,Y)

be an ordinary smooth function. Let
p\in[P]
, and define
f_{\varepsilon}:=\varphi(p_{\varepsilon},-)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(X,Y)
,
then
[f_{\varepsilon}(-)]:[X]\longrightarrow[Y]
is a GSF. In particular,
if
f:[X]\longrightarrow[Y]
is a GSF defined by
(f_{\varepsilon})
and
the net
(f_{\varepsilon})
is smooth in
\varepsilon
, i.e. if

\exists\varphi\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}((0,1)\times X,Y):\ f_{\varepsilon}=\varphi(\varepsilon,-)\quad\forall\varepsilon\in(0,1),

and if
[\varepsilon]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, then the GSF
f
is of Cauchy-Dirac type
because
f(x)=\varphi([\varepsilon],x)
for all
x\in[X]
. Finally, Cauchy-Dirac
GSF are closed with respect to composition.

Example 22
.

The composition
\delta\circ\delta\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}},\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})

is given by
(\delta\circ\delta)(x)=b\mu\left(b^{2}\mu(bx)\right)

and is an even function. If
x
is near-standard and
{x^{\circ}}\neq 0
,
or
x
is infinite, then
(\delta\circ\delta)(x)=b
. Since
(\delta\circ\delta)(0)=0
,
by the intermediate value theorem (see Cor.
33

below), we have that
\delta\circ\delta
attains any value in the
interval
[0,b]\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. If
0\leq x\leq\frac{1}{2b}
, then
(for a
\mu
as in Fig.
3.1
)
x
is infinitesimal
and
(\delta\circ\delta)(x)=0
because
\delta(x)\geq b\mu\left(\frac{1}{k}\right)

is an infinite number. If
x=\frac{k}{b}
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
,
then
x
is still infinitesimal but
(\delta\circ\delta)(x)=b
because

\mu(bx)=0
. A representation of
\delta\circ\delta
is given in
Fig.
3.2
. Analogously, one can deal with
H\circ\delta

and
\delta\circ H
.

Figure 3.2.
A representation of
\delta\circ\delta

The theory of GSF originates from the theory of Colombeau quotient
algebras. In this well-developed approach, strong analytic tools,
including microlocal analysis, and an elaborate theory of pseudodifferential
and Fourier integral operators have been developed over the past few
years (cf.
[
13
,
34
]
and references therein). As we already
mentioned above, in these quotient algebras, each generalized function
generates a unique GSF defined on a subset of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(\varepsilon)$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$(\varepsilon)$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$(\varepsilon)$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$(\varepsilon)$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. On the
other hand, Colombeau GF are in general not closed with respect to
composition, see e.g.
[
34
]
.

Similarly, we can define generalized functions of class
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{k}
,
with
k\leq+\infty
:

Definition 23
.

Let
X\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
Y\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
be arbitrary
subsets of generalized points and
k\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{+\infty\}
. Then we
say that

f:X\longrightarrow Y\text{ is a \emph{generalized }}\mathcal{C}^{k}\text{ \emph{function}}

if there exists a net
f_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{C}^{k}(\Omega_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{R}^{d})

defining
f
in the sense that

(i)

X\subseteq\langle\Omega_{\varepsilon}\rangle
,

(ii)

f([x_{\varepsilon}])=[f_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})]\in Y
for all
x=[x_{\varepsilon}]\in X
,

(iii)

(\partial^{\alpha}f_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon}))\in\mathbb{R}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}^{d}

for all
x=[x_{\varepsilon}]\in X
and all
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
such that

|\alpha|\leq k
.

(iv)

\forall\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\,\forall[x_{\varepsilon}],[x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]\in X:\ |\alpha|=k,\ [x_{\varepsilon}]=[x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]\ \Rightarrow\ [\partial^{\alpha}f_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})]=[\partial^{\alpha}f_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon})]
.

(v)

For all
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
, with
|\alpha|=k
, the
map
[x_{\varepsilon}]\in X\mapsto[\partial^{\alpha}f_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}

is continuous in the sharp topology.

The space of generalized
\mathcal{C}^{k}
functions from
X
to

Y
is denoted by
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{k}(X,Y)
.

Note that properties
(iv)
,
(v)
are required
only for
|\alpha|=k
because for lower length they can be proved
using property
(iii)
and the classical mean value theorem
for
f_{\varepsilon}
(see e.g.
[
31
]
). From Thm.
15

and Thm.
16
.
(ii)
it follows
that this definition of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{k}
is equivalent to Def.
13

if
k=+\infty
. Moreover, properties similar to
(iii)

and Thm.
20
can also be proved for
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{k}
.

Note that the absolute value function
|-|:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
is not a
GSF because its derivative is not sharply continuous at the origin;
clearly, it is a
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{0}
function.

3.4.
Differential calculus of GSF

In this section we show how the derivatives of a GSF can be calculated
using a form of incremental ratio. The idea is to prove the Fermat-Reyes
theorem for GSF (see
[
31
,
29
,
42
]
). Essentially,
this theorem shows the existence and uniqueness of another GSF serving
as incremental ratio. This is the first of a long list of results
demonstrating the close similarities between ordinary smooth functions
and GSF.

In the present setting, the Fermat-Reyes theorem (also called
Carathéodory definition of derivative) is the following.

Theorem 24
.

Let
U\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be
a sharply open set, let
v=[v_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{+\infty\}
,
and let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{k+1}(U,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
be a
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{k+1}
map generated
by the net of functions
f_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{R})
.
Then

(i)

There exists a sharp neighborhood
T

of
U\times\{0\}
and a map
r\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{k}(T,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
, called the

generalized incremental ratio
of
f

along

v
, such
that

\forall(x,h)\in T:\ f(x+hv)=f(x)+h\cdot r(x,h).

(ii)

Any two generalized incremental ratios
coincide on a sharp neighborhood of
U\times\{0\}
, so that we can
use the notation
\frac{\partial f}{\partial v}[x;h]:=r(x,h)
if

(x,h)
are sufficiently small.

(iii)

We have
\frac{\partial f}{\partial v}[x;0]=\left[\frac{\partial f_{\varepsilon}}{\partial v_{\varepsilon}}(x_{\varepsilon})\right]

for every
x\in U
and we can thus define
df(x)\cdot v:=\frac{\partial f}{\partial v}(x):=\frac{\partial f}{\partial v}[x;0]
,
so that
\frac{\partial f}{\partial v}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{k}(U,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
.

Note that this result allows us to consider the partial derivative
of
f
with respect to an arbitrary generalized vector
v\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}

which can be, e.g., near-standard or infinite. Since any partial derivative
of a GSF is still a GSF, higher order derivatives
\frac{\partial^{\alpha}f}{\partial v^{\alpha}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(U,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})

are simply defined recursively.

As follows from Thm.
24
.
(i)

and Thm.
18
.
(v)
, the concept
of derivative defined using the Fermat-Reyes theorem is compatible
with the classical derivative of Schwartz distributions via the embeddings

\iota^{b}
from Thm.
18
. The following result
follows from the analogous properties for the nets of smooth functions
defining
f
and
g
or directly from the Fermat-Reyes Thm.
24
.

Theorem 25
.

Let
U\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be an
open subset in the sharp topology, let
v\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
f
,

g:U\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
be generalized smooth maps. Then

(i)

\frac{\partial(f+g)}{\partial v}=\frac{\partial f}{\partial v}+\frac{\partial g}{\partial v}

(ii)

\frac{\partial(r\cdot f)}{\partial v}=r\cdot\frac{\partial f}{\partial v}\quad\forall r\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

(iii)

\frac{\partial(f\cdot g)}{\partial v}=\frac{\partial f}{\partial v}\cdot g+f\cdot\frac{\partial g}{\partial v}

(iv)

For each
x\in U
, the map
\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}f}{\hbox{\rm d}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}f$}}(x).v:=\frac{\partial f}{\partial v}(x)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

is
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
-linear in
v\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

(v)

Let
V\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
be open subsets in the sharp topology
and
h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(V,U)
be a generalized smooth maps. Then for all
x\in V

and all
v\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\left(f\circ h\right)}{\partial v}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}f}{\hbox{\rm d}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}f$}}\left(h(x)\right).\frac{\partial h}{\partial v}(x)

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\!\left(f\circ h\right)}{\hbox{\rm d}\!\left(f\circ h\right)}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\!\left(f\circ h\right)$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\!\left(f\circ h\right)$}}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}f}{\hbox{\rm d}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}f$}}\left(h(x)\right)\circ\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}h}{\hbox{\rm d}h}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}h$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}h$}}(x).

3.5.
Integral calculus using primitives

In this section, we inquire existence and uniqueness of primitives

F
of a GSF
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}([a,b],\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
. To this end, we shall have
to introduce the derivative
F^{\prime}(x)
at boundary points
x\in[a,b]
,
i.e. such that
x-a
or
b-x
is not invertible. Let us note explicitly,
in fact, that the Fermat-Reyes Theorem
24
is stated
only for sharply open domains.

The following result shows that every GSF can have at most one primitive
GSF up to an additive constant.

Theorem 26
.

Let
X\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
and
let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
be a generalized smooth function. Let
a
,

b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, with
a<b
, such that
(a,b)\subseteq X
. If
f^{\prime}(x)=0

for all
x\in\text{\emph{int}}(a,b)
, then
f
is constant on
(a,b)
.
An analogous statement holds if we take any other type of interval
(closed or half closed) instead of
(a,b)
.

Remark 27
.

From the Fermat-Reyes Thm.
24

and from Thm.
26
, it follows that
the function
i(x):=1
if
x\approx 0
and
i(x):=0
otherwise cannot
be a GSF on any large neighborhood of
x=0
. This example stems from
the property that different standard real numbers can always be separated
by infinitesimal balls.

At interior points
x\in[a,b]
in the sharp topology, the definition
of derivative
f^{(k)}(x)
follows from the Fermat-Reyes Theorem

24
. At boundary points, we have the following

Theorem 28
.

Let
a
,
b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

with
a<b
, and
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}([a,b],\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
be a generalized smooth
function. Then for all
x\in[a,b]
, the following limit exists in
the sharp topology

\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\to x\\
y\in\text{\emph{int}}\left([a,b]\right)\end{subarray}}f^{(k)}(y)=:f^{(k)}(x).

Moreover, if the net
f_{\varepsilon}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\Omega_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{R})
defines

f
and
x=[x_{\varepsilon}]
, then
f^{(k)}(x)=[f_{\varepsilon}^{(k)}(x_{\varepsilon})]

and hence
f^{(k)}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}([a,b],\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
.

We can now state existence and uniqueness of primitives of GSF:

Theorem 29
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{+\infty\}

and
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{k}([a,b],\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
be defined in the interval
[a,b]\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
where
a<b
. Let
c\in[a,b]
. Then, there exists one and only one
generalized
\mathcal{C}^{k+1}
map
F\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{k+1}([a,b],\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})

such that
F(c)=0
and
F^{\prime}(x)=f(x)
for all
x\in[a,b]
. Moreover,
if
f
is defined by the net
f_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{C}^{k}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R})

and
c=[c_{\varepsilon}]
, then
F(x)=\left[\int_{c_{\varepsilon}}^{x_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}(s)\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}s}{\hbox{\rm d}s}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}s$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}s$}}\right]

for all
x=[x_{\varepsilon}]\in[a,b]
.

Definition 30
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
29
,
we denote by
\int_{c}^{(-)}f:=\int_{c}^{(-)}f(s)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}s}{\hbox{\rm d}s}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}s$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}s$}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}([a,b],\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})

the unique generalized smooth function such that:

(i)

\int_{c}^{c}f=0

(ii)

\left(\int_{c}^{(-)}f\right)^{\prime}(x)=\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}}{\hbox{\rm d}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}$}}}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}}\int_{c}^{x}f(s)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}s}{\hbox{\rm d}s}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}s$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}s$}}=f(x)

for all
x\in[a,b]
.

To consider a generalization of this concept of integration to GSF
in several variables and to more general domains of integration
M\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,
see Sec.
3.7
and
[
31
]
.

Example 31
.

(i)

Since
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
contains both infinitesimal and infinite numbers,
our notion of definite integral also includes “improper integrals”.
Let e.g.
f(x)=\frac{1}{x}
for
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
and
a=1
,
b=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-q}
,

q>0
. Then

\int_{a}^{b}f(s)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}s}{\hbox{\rm d}s}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}s$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}s$}}=\left[\int_{1}^{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-q}}\frac{1}{s}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}s}{\hbox{\rm d}s}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}s$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}s$}}\right]=[\log\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-q}]-\log 1=-q\log\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}},

(3.7)

which is, of course, a positive infinite generalized number. This
apparently trivial result is closely tied to the possibility to define
GSF on arbitrary domains, like
F\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}([a,b],\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
in Thm.
29

where
b
is an infinite number as in (
3.7
), which
is one of the key properties allowing one to get the closure with
respect to composition.

(ii)

If
p
,
q\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
p<0<q
and both
p
and
q
are not infinitesimal,
then
\int_{p}^{q}\delta(t)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}\approx 1
. If
p\leq-r
and

q\geq s
where
r
,
s\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, then
\int_{p}^{q}\delta(t)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}=1
.

Theorem 32
.

Let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
and
g\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(Y,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})

be generalized smooth functions defined on arbitrary domains in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.
Let
a
,
b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
with
a<b
and
[a,b]\subseteq X\cap Y
,
then

(i)

\int_{a}^{b}\left(f+g\right)=\int_{a}^{b}f+\int_{a}^{b}g

(ii)

\int_{a}^{b}\lambda f=\lambda\int_{a}^{b}f\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

(iii)

\int_{a}^{b}f=\int_{a}^{c}f+\int_{c}^{b}f

for all
c\in[a,b]

(iv)

\int_{a}^{b}f=-\int_{b}^{a}f

(v)

\int_{a}^{b}f^{\prime}=f(b)-f(a)

(vi)

\int_{a}^{b}f^{\prime}\cdot g=\left[f\cdot g\right]_{a}^{b}-\int_{a}^{b}f\cdot g^{\prime}

(vii)

If
f(x)\leq g(x)
for all
x\in[a,b]
, then

\int_{a}^{b}f\leq\int_{a}^{b}g
.

Let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(T,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
and
\varphi\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(S,T)

be generalized smooth functions defined on arbitrary domains in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.
Let
a
,
b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, with
a<b
, such that
[a,b]\subseteq S
,

\varphi(a)<\varphi(b)
and
[\varphi(a),\varphi(b)]\subseteq T
. Finally, assume
that
\varphi([a,b])\subseteq[\varphi(a),\varphi(b)]
. Then

\int_{\varphi(a)}^{\varphi(b)}f(t)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}=\int_{a}^{b}f\left[\varphi(s)\right]\cdot\varphi^{\prime}(s)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}s}{\hbox{\rm d}s}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}s$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}s$}}.

3.6.
Classical theorems for GSF

It is natural to expect that several classical theorems of differential
and integral calculus can be extended from the ordinary smooth case
to the generalized smooth framework. Once again, we underscore that
these faithful generalizations are possible because we do not have
a priori limitations in the evaluation
f(x)
for GSF. For example,
one does not have similar results in Colombeau theory, where an arbitrary
generalized function can be evaluated only at compactly supported
points.

We start from the intermediate value theorem.

Corollary 33
.

Let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
be a generalized
smooth function defined on the subset
X\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. Let
a
,

b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, with
a<b
, such that
[a,b]\subseteq X
. Assume
that
f(a)<f(b)
. Then

\forall y\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}:\ f(a)\leq y\leq f(b)\ \Rightarrow\ \exists c\in[a,b]:\ y=f(c).

Using this theorem we can conclude that no GSF can assume
only a finite number of values which are comparable with respect to
the relation
<
on any nontrivial interval
[a,b]\subseteq X
,
unless it is constant. For example, this provides an alternative way
of seeing that the function
i
of Rem.
27
cannot
be a generalized smooth map.

We note that the solution
c\in[a,b]
of the previous generalized
smooth equation
y=f(x)
need not even be continuous in
\varepsilon
,
see e.g.
[
31
]
for an explicit counter example. This
allows us to draw the following general conclusion: if we consider
generalized numbers as solutions of smooth equations, then we are
forced to work on a non-totally ordered ring of scalars derived from
discontinuous (in
\varepsilon
) representatives. To put it differently:
if we choose a ring of scalars with a total order or continuous representatives,
we will not be able to solve every smooth equation, and the given
ring can be considered, in some sense, incomplete.

The next theorem deals with different version of the mean value theorem

Theorem 34
.

Let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
be a generalized
smooth function defined in the sharply open set
X\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
Let
a
,
b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
such that
[a,b]\subseteq X
. Then

(i)

If
n=d=1
, then
\exists c\in[a,b]:\ f(b)-f(a)=(b-a)\cdot f^{\prime}(c)
.

(ii)

If
n=d=1
, then
\exists c\in[a,b]:\ \int_{a}^{b}f(t)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}=(b-a)\cdot f(c)
.

(iii)

If
d=1
, then
\exists c\in[a,b]:\ f(b)-f(a)=\nabla f(c)\cdot(b-a)
.

(iv)

Let
h:=b-a
, then
f(a+h)-f(a)=\int_{0}^{1}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}f}{\hbox{\rm d}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}f$}}(a+t\cdot h).h\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}
.

Internal and sharply bounded sets generated by a net of compact sets
serve as a substitute for compact subsets for GSF, as can be seen
from the following extreme value theorem:

Lemma 35
.

Let
\emptyset\neq K=[K_{\varepsilon}]\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}

be an internal set generated by compact sets
K_{\varepsilon}\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}

such that
K
is
sharply bounded
, i.e.
K\subseteq B_{R}(0)

for some
R\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
. Assume that
\alpha:K\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
is a well-defined
map given by
\alpha(x)=[\alpha_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})]
for all
x\in K
,
where
\alpha_{\varepsilon}:K_{\varepsilon}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
are continuous maps (e.g.
\alpha(x)=|x|
).
Then

\exists m,M\in K\,\forall x\in K:\ \alpha(m)\leq\alpha(x)\leq\alpha(M).

Corollary 36
.

Let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
be a generalized
smooth function defined in the subset
X\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Let

\emptyset\neq K=[K_{\varepsilon}]\subseteq X
be as above, then

\exists m,M\in K\,\forall x\in K:\ f(m)\leq f(x)\leq f(M).

(3.8)

These results motivate the following

Definition 37
.

A subset
K
of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
is called

functionally compact
, denoted by
K\Subset_{\text{f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, if there
exists a net
(K_{\varepsilon})
such that

(i)

K=[K_{\varepsilon}]\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}

(ii)

K
is sharply bounded, i.e.
\exists R\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}:\ K\subseteq B_{R}(0)

(iii)

\forall\varepsilon\in I:\ K_{\varepsilon}\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}

If, in addition,
K\subseteq U\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
then we write

K\Subset_{\text{f}}U
. Any net
(K_{\varepsilon})
such that
[K_{\varepsilon}]=K
is called
a
representative
of
K
.

We motivate the name
functionally compact subset

by noting that on this type of subsets, GSF have properties very similar
to those that ordinary smooth functions have on standard compact sets.

Remark 38
.

(i)

By Thm.
11
.
(iii)
,
any internal set
K=[K_{\varepsilon}]
is closed in the sharp topology.
Therefore, functionally compact sets are sharply closed and bounded
subsets of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. In particular, the open interval
(0,1)\subseteq\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

is not functionally compact since it is not closed.

(ii)

If
H\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
is
a non-empty ordinary compact set, then the internal set
[H]
is
functionally compact. In particular,
[0,1]=\left[[0,1]_{\mathbb{R}}\right]

is functionally compact.

(iii)

The empty set
\emptyset=\widetilde{\emptyset}\Subset_{\text{f}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.

(iv)

\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
is not functionally
compact since it is not sharply bounded.

(v)

The set of finite points

{\text{\rm c}}({\mathbb{R}})
is not functionally compact because the GSF
f(x)=x

does not satisfy the conclusion (
3.8
) of Cor.
36
.

We also underscore the following properties of functionally
compact sets.

Theorem 39
.

Let
K\subseteq X\subseteq\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{k}(X,\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
.
Then
K\Subset_{\text{f}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
implies
f(K)\Subset_{\text{f}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.

As a corollary of this theorem and Rem. (
38
).
(ii)

we get

Corollary 40
.

If
a
,
b\in\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
and
a\leq b
,
then
[a,b]\Subset_{\text{f}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.

Let us note that
a
,
b\in\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
can also be infinite
numbers, e.g.
a=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-N}
,
b=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-M}
or
a=-\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-N}
,

b=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-M}
with
M
,
N\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
, so that e.g.
[-\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-N},\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{M}]\supseteq\mathbb{R}
.
Therefore, despite very similar properties shared by functionally
compact sets and classical compact sets, the former can also be unbounded
from the classical point of view.

Finally, in the following result we consider the product
of functionally compact sets:

Theorem 41
.

Let
K\Subset_{\text{f}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
H\Subset_{\text{f}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,
then
K\times H\Subset_{\text{f}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n+d}
. In particular, if
a_{i}\leq b_{i}

for
i=1,\ldots,n
, then
\prod_{i=1}^{n}[a_{i},b_{i}]\Subset_{\text{f}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

A theory of compactly supported GSF has been developed in
[
28
]
,
and it closely resembles the classical theory of LF-spaces of compactly
supported smooth functions. It establishes that for suitable functionally
compact subsets, the corresponding space of compactly supported GSF
contains extensions of all Colombeau generalized functions, and hence
also of all Schwartz distributions.

Note also that any interval
[a,b]\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
with
b-a\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
,
is functionally compact but
not
connected: in fact if
c\in(a,b)
,
then both
c+D_{\infty}
and
[a,b]\setminus\left(c+D_{\infty}\right)

are sharply open in
[a,b]
. Once again, this is a general property
in several non-Archimedean frameworks (see e.g.
[
66
,
42
]
).
On the other hand, as in the case of functionally compact sets, GSF
behave on intervals as if they were connected, in the sense that both
the intermediate value theorem Cor.
33
and the
extreme value theorem Cor.
36
hold for them
(therefore,
f\left([a,b]\right)=\left[f(m),f(M)\right]
, where we
used the notations from the results just mentioned).

We close this section with generalizations of Taylor’s theorem in
various forms. In the following statement,
\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{k}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{k}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{k}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{k}f$}}(x):\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{dk}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

is the
k
-th differential of the GSF
f
, viewed as an
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
-multilinear
map
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\displaystyle\mathop{\ldots\ldots\,}^{k}\times\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, and we use the
common notation
\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{k}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{k}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{k}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{k}f$}}(x)\cdot h^{k}:=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{k}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{k}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{k}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{k}f$}}(x)(h,\ldots,h)
.
Clearly,
\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{k}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{k}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{k}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{k}f$}}(x)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{dk},\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
. For multilinear
maps
A:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{p}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{q}
, we set
|A|:=[|A_{\varepsilon}|]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
the generalized number defined by the norms of the operators
A_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{R}^{p}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{q}
.

Theorem 42
.

Let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(U,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
be a generalized
smooth function defined in the sharply open set
U\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
Let
a
,
b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
such that the line segment
[a,b]\subseteq U
,
and set
h:=b-a
. Then, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have

(i)

\exists\xi\in[a,b]:\ f(a+h)=\sum_{j=0}^{n}\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{j}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{j}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{j}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{j}f$}}(a)}{j!}\cdot h^{j}+\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f$}}(\xi)}{(n+1)!}\cdot h^{n+1}.

(ii)

f(a+h)=\sum_{j=0}^{n}\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{j}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{j}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{j}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{j}f$}}(a)}{j!}\cdot h^{j}+\frac{1}{n!}\cdot\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{n}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f$}}(a+th)\cdot h^{n+1}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}.

Moreover, there exists some
R\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
such that

\forall k\in B_{R}(0)\,\exists\xi\in[a,a+k]:\ f(a+k)=\sum_{j=0}^{n}\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{j}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{j}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{j}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{j}f$}}(a)}{j!}\cdot k^{j}+\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f$}}(\xi)}{(n+1)!}\cdot k^{n+1}

(3.9)

\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f$}}(\xi)}{(n+1)!}\cdot k^{n+1}=\frac{1}{n!}\cdot\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{n}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f$}}(a+tk)\cdot k^{n+1}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}\approx 0.

(3.10)

Formulas
(i)
and
(ii)

correspond to a plain generalization of Taylor’s theorem for ordinary
smooth functions with Lagrange and integral remainder, respectively.
Dealing with GF, it is important to note that this direct statement
also includes the possibility that the differential
\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f$}}(\xi)

may be infinite at some point. For this reason, in (
3.9
)
and (
3.10
), considering a sufficiently small
increment
k
, we get more classical infinitesimal remainders
\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f}{\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}^{n+1}f$}}(\xi)\cdot k^{n+1}\approx 0
.

The following definitions allow us to state Taylor formulas in Peano
and in infinitesimal form. The latter has no remainder term thanks
to the use of an equivalence relation that permits the introduction
of a language of nilpotent infinitesimals, see e.g.
[
25
,
26
]

for a similar formulation. For simplicity, we only present the 1-dimentional
case.

Definition 43
.

(i)

Let
U\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
be a sharp neighborhood
of
0
and
P
,
Q:U\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
be maps defined on
U
.
Then we say that

P(u)=o(Q(u))\quad\text{as }u\to 0

if there exists a function
R:U\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\forall u\in U:\ P(u)=R(u)\cdot Q(u)\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{u\to 0}R(u)=0,

where the limit is taken in the sharp topology.

(ii)

Let
x
,
y\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

and
k
,
j\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, then we write
x=_{j}y
if there exist
representatives
(x_{\varepsilon})
,
(y_{\varepsilon})
of
x
,
y
, respectively,
such that

|x_{\varepsilon}-y_{\varepsilon}|=O(\rho_{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{j}}).

(3.11)

We will read
x=_{j}y
as
x

is equal to

y

up to

j
-th order infinitesimals
. Finally, if
k\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
, we
set
D_{kj}:=\left\{x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mid x^{k+1}=_{j}0\right\}
, which
is called the
set of

k
-th order infinitesimals for
the equality
=_{j}
, and

D_{\infty j}:=\left\{x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mid\exists k\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}:\ x^{k+1}=_{j}0\right\}

which is called the
set of infinitesimals for the equality
=_{j}
.

Of course, the reformulation of Def.
43

(i)
for the classical Landau’s little-oh is particularly
suited to the case of a ring like
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, instead of a field. The
intuitive interpretation of
x=_{j}y
is that for particular (e.g. physics-related)
problems one is not interested in distinguishing quantities whose
difference
|x-y|
is less than an infinitesimal of order
j
. In
fact, if
x=_{j}y
we can write
x_{\varepsilon}=y_{\varepsilon}+r_{\varepsilon}
with

r_{\varepsilon}\to 0
of order at most
\rho_{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{j}}
. The
idea behind taking
\frac{1}{j}
in (
3.11
)
is to obtain the property that the greater the order
j
of the infinitesimal
error, the greater the difference
|x-y|
is allowed to be. This
is a typical property in rings with nilpotent infinitesimals (see
e.g.
[
25
,
42
]
). The set
D_{ki}
represents the neighborhood
of infinitesimals of
k
-th order for the equality
=_{j}
. Once
again, the greater the order
k
, the bigger is the neighborhood
(see Theorem
44
.
(ix)

below). Note that if
x=_{j}y
, then
x_{\varepsilon}=y_{\varepsilon}+o\left(\rho_{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{j}-a}\right)

for all
a\in(0,1/j]_{\mathbb{R}}
. In particular,
x_{\varepsilon}=y_{\varepsilon}+o\left(\rho_{\varepsilon}\right)

implies
x=_{1}y
, whereas
x=_{1}y
yields only
x_{\varepsilon}=y_{\varepsilon}+o\left(\rho_{\varepsilon}^{1-a}\right)

for all
a\in(0,1]_{\mathbb{R}}
. Finally, note that
x=_{j}y
is equivalent
to
|x-y|\leq C\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{\frac{1}{j}}
for some
C\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.

Theorem 44
.

Let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(U,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
be
a generalized smooth function defined in the sharply open set
U\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.
Let
x
,
\delta\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, with
\delta>0
and
[x-\delta,x+\delta]\subseteq U
.
Let
k
,
l
,
j\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
. Then

(i)

\forall n\in\mathbb{N}:\ f(x+u)=\sum_{r=0}^{n}\frac{f^{(r)}(x)}{r!}u^{r}+o(u^{n})

as
u\to 0
.

(ii)

The definition of
x=_{j}y
does not depend
on the representatives of
x
,
y
.

(iii)

=_{j}
is an equivalence relation on

\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.

(iv)

If
x=_{j}y
and
l\geq j
,
then
x=_{l}y
. Therefore,
D_{nj}\subseteq D_{nl}
.

(v)

If
x=_{j}y
for all
j\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
sufficiently
small, then
x=y
.

(vi)

If
x=_{j}y
and
z=_{j}w
then
x+z=_{j}y+w
.
If
x
and
z
are finite, then
x\cdot z=_{j}y\cdot w
.

(vii)

If
x=_{j}y
,
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}([a,b],\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
,

x
,
y\in[a,b]
, and
f^{\prime}(c)
is finite for all
c\in[a,b]
, then

f(x)=_{j}f(y)
.

(viii)

\forall h\in D_{kj}:\ h\approx 0
.

(ix)

D_{mj}\subseteq D_{kj}\subseteq D_{\infty j}

if
m\leq k
.

(x)

D_{kj}
is a subring of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.
For all
h\in D_{kj}
and all finite
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, we have
x\cdot h\in D_{kj}
.

(xi)

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
and assume that
j
, k and

f
satisfy

\forall z\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\,\forall\xi\in[x-\delta,x+\delta]:\ z=_{j}0\ \Rightarrow\ z\cdot f^{(n+1)}(\xi)=_{k}0.

(3.12)

Then, we have

\forall u\in D_{nj}:\ f(x+u)=_{k}\sum_{r=0}^{n}\frac{f^{(r)}(x)}{r!}u^{r}.

(xii)

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
there exist
e\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}

such that
e\leq j
, and
\forall u\in D_{ne}:\ f(x+u)=_{j}\sum_{r=0}^{n}\frac{f^{(r)}(x)}{r!}u^{r}
.

We shall use the nilpotent Taylor formula
(xii)

in Sec.
5
for the deduction of the heat
and wave equation for GSF; we therefore note here that the index
e

depends on the GSF
f
: in that case, we say that
the nilpotent
Taylor formula of order
n
holds for
f
on
D_{ne}
. From
(iv)

it hence follows that it also holds on
D_{ne^{\prime}}
for all
e^{\prime}\leq e
.

3.7.
Multidimensional integration

Finally, we can also introduce multidimensional integration of GSF
on suitable subsets of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

Definition 45
.

Let
\mu
be a measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
let
K
be a functionally compact subset of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Then,
we call
K

\mu
-
measurable
if the limit

\mu(K):=\lim_{m\to\infty}[\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon}))]

(3.13)

exists for some representative
(K_{\varepsilon})
of
K
. Here
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
the limit is taken in the sharp topology on
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
since
[\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon}))]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
and
\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{r}(A):=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:d(x,A)\leq r\}
.

In the following result, we show that this definition generates
a correct notion of multidimensional integration for GSF.

Theorem 46
.

Let
K\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}

be
\mu
-measurable.

(i)

The definition of
\mu(K)
is independent of
the representative
(K_{\varepsilon})
.

(ii)

There exists a representative
(K_{\varepsilon})

of
K
such that
\mu(K)=[\mu(K_{\varepsilon})]
.

(iii)

Let
(K_{\varepsilon})
be any representative
of
K
and let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
be a GSF defined by the net

(f_{\varepsilon})
. Then

\int_{K}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}:=\lim_{m\to\infty}\biggl[\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\biggr]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

exists and its value is independent of the representative
(K_{\varepsilon})
.

(iv)

There exists a representative
(K_{\varepsilon})

of
K
such that

\int_{K}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}=\biggl[\int_{K_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\biggr]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

(3.14)

for each
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
.

(v)

If
K=\prod_{i=1}^{n}[a_{i},b_{i}]
, then
K
is
\lambda
-measurable
(
\lambda
being the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
) and for all
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})

we have

\int_{K}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}=\left[\int_{a_{1,\varepsilon}}^{b_{1,\varepsilon}}\,dx_{1}\dots\int_{a_{n,\varepsilon}}^{b_{n,\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

for any representatives
(a_{i,\varepsilon})
,
(b_{i,\varepsilon})
of
a_{i}

and
b_{i}
respectively. Therefore, if
n=1
, this notion of integral
coincides with that of Thm.
29

and Def.
30
.

(vi)

Let
K\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be
\lambda
-measurable, where
\lambda

is the Lebesgue measure, and let
\varphi\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
be
such that
\varphi^{-1}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\varphi(K),\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
. Then
\varphi(K)

is
\lambda
-measurable and

\int_{\varphi(K)}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}=\int_{K}(f\circ\varphi)\left|\det(\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi}{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi$}})\right|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}

for each
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\varphi(K),\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
.

4.
Differential equations: the
Picard-Lindelöf theorem for ODE

As in the classical case, thanks to the extreme value Lem.
35

and the properties of functionally compact sets
K
, we can naturally
define a topology on the space
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{k}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
:

Definition 47
.

Let
K\Subset_{\text{f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be a functionally
compact set such that
K=\overline{\accentset{\circ}{K}}
(so that
partial derivatives at sharply boundary points can be defined as limits
of partial derivatives at sharply interior points; such
K
are called

solid
sets). Let
l\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq k}
and
v\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{k}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
.
Then

\|v\|_{l}:=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|\leq l\\
1\leq i\leq d\end{subarray}}\max\left(\left|\partial^{\alpha}v^{i}(M_{ni})\right|,\left|\partial^{\alpha}v^{i}(m_{ni})\right|\right)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}},

where
M_{ni}
,
m_{ni}\in K
satisfy

\forall x\in K:\ \partial^{\alpha}v^{i}(m_{ni})\leq\partial^{\alpha}v^{i}(x)\leq\partial^{\alpha}v^{i}(M_{ni}).

The following result permits us to calculate the (generalized)
norm
\|v\|_{l}
using any net
(v_{\varepsilon})
that defines

v
.

Lemma 48
.

Under the assumptions of Def.
47
,
let
[K_{\varepsilon}]=K\Subset_{\text{f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be any representative of
K
.
Then we have:

(i)

If the net
(v_{\varepsilon})
defines
v
, then

\|v\|_{l}=\left[\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|\leq l\\
1\leq i\leq d\end{subarray}}\max_{x\in K_{\varepsilon}}\left|\partial^{\alpha}v_{\varepsilon}^{i}(x)\right|\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
;

(ii)

\|v\|_{l}\geq 0
;

(iii)

\|v\|_{l}=0
if and only if
v=0
;

(iv)

\forall c\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}:\ \|c\cdot v\|_{l}=|c|\cdot\|v\|_{l}
;

(v)

For all
u\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{k}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
, we have

\|u+v\|_{l}\leq\|u\|_{l}+\|v\|_{l}
and
\|u\cdot v\|_{l}\leq c_{l}\cdot\|u\|_{l}\cdot\|v\|_{l}

for some
c_{l}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
.

Using these
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
